Solob Wrapper ? Version 1 (Redrafted)
Foundations with Anchor Primacy
Purpose of V1

1. Core Principle of Version 1
Nothing enters memory without an Anchor
The Anchor Library is not a feature ?
it is the load-bearing wall.

Every note, chunk, embedding, and answer must be traceable to an anchor.

If it cannot be anchored -> it stays Shimmer or returns UNKNOWN.

2. The Anchor Library (Mandatory in V1)
2.1 What an Anchor Is
An Anchor is a grounded reference object that stabilizes meaning.

Examples:

A document (Book draft, PDF, article)

A canon note you wrote

A verified transcript

A dataset

A sealed definition

Anchors answer:

?Where did this come from, and why should I trust it??

2.2 Anchor Object (V1 Schema ? minimal)
Anchor {
  anchor_id: string,
  title: string,
  type: "document" | "note" | "dataset" | "definition",
  source_path: string,
  created_at: timestamp,
  checksum: string,
  status: "canon" | "working" | "shimmer"
}
? Rule:
Chunks, embeddings, and answers must reference anchor_id.

No orphan thoughts. No free-floating truth.

3. Version 1 Data Spine (Minimal but Correct)
SQLite (portable, offline, sacred simplicity).

3.1 Tables (V1 Minimum)
anchors
The root of truth.

field	purpose
anchor_id	primary key
title	human name
type	document / note / dataset
source_path	file reference
checksum	integrity
status	canon / working / shimmer
notes
Raw human input.

field	purpose
note_id	primary key
anchor_id	required
text	raw note
tags	optional
created_at	timestamp
chunks
Parsed meaning units.

field	purpose
chunk_id	primary key
anchor_id	required
note_id	optional
text	chunk text
position	order
created_at	timestamp
embeddings
Vectors with lineage.

field	purpose
embedding_id	primary key
chunk_id	required
model	embedding model
vector	stored vector
(or inline vector field on chunks for simplicity)

runs
Every answer ever given.

field	purpose
run_id	primary key
query	user input
answer	model output
cited_chunks	list of chunk_ids
cited_anchors	derived
created_at	timestamp
verdict	ok / unknown / flagged
4. Ingestion Flow (V1)
Order matters. This is sacred.

File / Note
   ?
Create Anchor
   ?
Parse -> Chunks
   ?
Embed Chunks
   ?
Store with Anchor lineage
[WARN] If no anchor exists -> ingestion halts.

This prevents:

Memory pollution

Retroactive hallucination

?I think I remember? bugs

5. Retrieval + RAG (Version 1 Scope)
5.1 Retrieval Rules
Query -> embedding search

Top-K chunks returned

All chunks must resolve to ?1 anchor

5.2 Answer Rules
The system may only:

Answer using retrieved chunks

Cite anchors + chunks

Or respond: UNKNOWN

No creative filling.
No ?probably?.
No vibes pretending to be facts.

You said:
good question , any truth can change really but change in what way ? in meaning change ? in additional context change ? in reality change ? in order of events change ? in effect change ? in perspective change ? in relation change ? 
ChatGPT said:
Yeahhh?that?s the right interrogation. Because ?truth can change? is either deep wisdom? or the loophole that lets nonsense walk through the front door wearing a tux. ?

So let?s slice it properly.

A ?truth? can change along different axes, and each axis needs different rules in your system (especially for Anchors). Think of truth like a gem: rotate it and the light changes, but the stone may or may not.

The 6 main ways ?truth? changes (and what kind of change it is)
1) Meaning change (semantic drift)
What changes: the definition of a term or statement.
Example: ?Solob? initially means ?truth resonance,? later becomes ?a governing physics.?
Risk: high ? this is where Barber becomes Mason without anyone noticing.
System handling: versioned definitions + ?meaning at time-of-writing.?

Rule idea: A term?s meaning is allowed to evolve, but every usage must bind to a definition version.

2) Additional context change (context expansion)
What changes: the surrounding facts you later learn.
Example: You wrote ?Akae Beka represents ORON,? later you learn he?s ?the late? Vaughn Benjamin and that changes the weight, not necessarily the claim.
Risk: medium ? context can refine without contradicting.
System handling: append-only annotations (?context layers?), not edits.

Rule idea: Original line stays; new context attaches as a layer.

3) Reality change (the world actually changes)
What changes: the underlying state of the world.
Example: ?This caf? is open 24/7? becomes false because the caf? changes hours.
Risk: low philosophically, high operationally.
System handling: add validity windows (?true from? to??).

Rule idea: Claims about the external world require timestamps + expiry logic.

4) Order of events change (chronology correction)
What changes: the sequence you thought happened.
Example: You logged Idea A before Idea B, but later you find an old message proving B came first.
Risk: high for lineage/patents/history.
System handling: separate:

event_time (when it happened)

recorded_time (when you wrote it)

discovered_time (when you confirmed it)

Rule idea: Chronology can be corrected, but never silently rewritten?only re-indexed.

5) Effect change (impact revision)
What changes: the consequences you attribute to a truth.
Example: ?This habit helped me? later becomes ?it helped me short-term but harmed me long-term.?
Risk: medium ? effects are often lagging indicators.
System handling: treat effects as hypotheses with confidence, updated over time.

Rule idea: Effects are not facts; they are time-dependent evaluations.

6) Perspective change (observer shift)
What changes: the lens?emotional state, maturity, role, gate dominance.
Example: Same childhood memory feels different as a father.
Risk: low (if labeled), dangerous (if mistaken as new facts).
System handling: keep the ?fact? stable, store perspective metadata.

Rule idea: Perspective may change freely; it must be recorded as perspective, not overwrite the event.

You also named one more: relation change (and it?s a big one)
7) Relation change (graph rewiring)
What changes: how truths connect to other truths.
Example: A concept moves from ?ZAYN-coded? to ?VORAK-coded? after deeper mapping.
Risk: high for reasoning systems ? relations control inference.
System handling: build truth as a graph:

nodes = claims/concepts

edges = relations (support, contradict, derive, echo, metaphor-of)

Rule idea: Edges can change without deleting nodes; keep relation history.

The key: ?Truth? is not one thing in your system
Your system needs to classify each statement as one of these:

Definition truth (meaning)

Historical truth (event record)

Empirical truth (world state)

Interpretive truth (perspective)

Causal truth (effect)

Relational truth (graph links)

Because each type has a different allowed mutation.

My opinion (skeptical but friendly):
Saying ?any truth can change? is only safe if you also say:

Truth changes are allowed, but they must declare what changed.

Otherwise you?re building a time machine that edits evidence.
And that?s how memory systems become propaganda machines by accident.

Practical move for Solob Wrapper V1 (simple, strong)
For every stored statement, add 3 fields:

truth_type: definition | event | empirical | interpretive | causal | relational

mutation_mode: append-only | versioned | time-bounded | re-index-only

confidence: 0?1 (optional in V1 but powerful)

That?s enough to prevent ?Barber=Mason? drift without making the system heavy.

If you want, I can turn this into a one-page Anchor Invariants draft that?s tight enough to paste into /config/anchor.invariants.md right now?written like a constitution, not a blog post.

Why JSON is the right spine format for Solob Wrapper

word

definition

example

etymology

usage frequency

Solobic references

nested meaning

optional fields

future expansion

versioning

annotations

multiple languages

confidence layers

CSV does none of that without hacks.

2. JSON respects Anchor evolution
You already know these entries will evolve:

definitions refine

examples multiply

etymologies get footnotes

usage frequency recalibrates

relations between words emerge

With JSON, you can do:

{
  "word": "Alignment",
  "definition": {
    "v1": "...",
    "v2": "..."
  },
  "examples": [

      "text": "...",
      "context": "personal",
      "date": "2023-04-10"
    }
  ]
}
With CSV?
You?d be fighting commas like a man wrestling a snake in tall grass. ?

LLMs parse JSON cleanly

You can validate it

You can diff it

You can load it directly into:

ingestion pipelines

SQLite

vectorizers

logic auditors

CSV is for humans.
JSON is for thinking systems.

anchors/

    ??? A/

    ??? ...

{
  "letter": "A",
  "entries": [
    {
      "word": "Alignment",

      }
    }
  ]
}

never safely regenerate JSON from CSV

One-way door logic.

? Lexicon Anchor Invariants
(Canonical ? Version 1)

Purpose
The Lexicon Anchor exists to preserve meaning, usage, and linguistic lineage across time without distortion.
Its role is archival and referential, not corrective or prescriptive.

These invariants define what may never break.

1. Append-Only Definitions
Invariant: Definitions are append-only.

A definition, once written, is never deleted.

Refinements are added as new versions or annotations, not replacements.

Earlier definitions remain valid as historical truth, even if later refined.

Rationale: Meaning evolves; history must not be rewritten.

2. Original Wording is Sacred
Invariant: Original wording is never overwritten.

Text captured at the time of entry must remain verbatim.

Spelling, grammar, patois, tone, and informality are preserved.

No automated ?cleanup,? normalization, or correction is allowed on original text.

Rationale: Language carries context; correction erases signal.

3. Corrections are Annotations, Not Edits
Invariant: Corrections are expressed as annotations.

Corrections must:

reference the original text

explain why the correction exists

never silently modify the source

An annotation may clarify, expand, or contextualize?but not erase.

Rationale: Truth is layered, not replaced.

4. Provenance is Mandatory
Invariant: Every lexicon entry must retain provenance.

Each entry must be traceable to:

original source file

row index (or equivalent)

import timestamp

No provenance -> no canonical status.

Rationale: Meaning without origin becomes folklore.

5. Lexicon Entries Are Non-Executable
Invariant: Lexicon content has no side effects.

Lexicon entries do not:

trigger logic

mutate state

influence execution paths

They may be referenced but never act.

Rationale: Language should inform systems, not secretly control them.

6. Context May Expand, Never Collapse
Invariant: Context can be added, not collapsed.

Additional examples, cultural notes, etymology updates are allowed.

Contextual expansion must not narrow the original meaning unless explicitly annotated as a reinterpretation.

Rationale: Expansion preserves optionality; collapse destroys it.

7. Lexicon is Language-Respecting
Invariant: Non-standard language is first-class.

Patois, dialects, slang, and hybrid forms are valid entries.

No entry is downgraded for being informal, regional, or oral in origin.

Rationale: A system that disrespects language disrespects people.

8. Human Authority Supersedes Automation
Invariant: Humans outrank tools.

Automated imports may suggest structure.

Only human review may:

promote entries

add interpretive annotations

mark canonical usage

Rationale: Tools preserve meaning; humans define it.

Closing Principle

The Lexicon Anchor exists to remember honestly, not to be correct.

ONLY if the original cell had no explicit year intent

Practically:

If the Date object has a year equal to Excel?s default (e.g. 1899/1900/1970), treat it as symbolic -> "April 10"

If it has a meaningful year, stringify full date -> "April 10, 2023"

This keeps:

symbolic calendar anchors clean

historical anchors expressive

You don?t need this now ? just log it as a V1.1 note.

3?? About the NaN issue (what went wrong)
Good catch asking this ? NaN bugs are quiet assassins.

What happened (most likely)
In pandas:

Empty Excel cells become NaN (float)

NaN:

is not None

is truthy-weird

breaks string ops silently

Common failure mode:

if word:
    first_letter = word[0]
If word is NaN:

if word may pass unexpectedly

word[0] explodes

or worse: string conversion turns it into "nan"

That?s how you get:

ghost rows

N.json

or entries that look real but aren?t

Why your fix now works
You correctly:

explicitly detected NaN

skipped rows where Word is NaN or empty

prevented NaN from ever reaching filename or grouping logic

That?s the right place to kill NaN: at ingestion.

4?? Canonical rule you should lock in (write this down)
NaN is not data.
NaN is absence masquerading as presence.

So your invariant becomes:

NaN is treated the same as:

empty string

missing cell

NaN never reaches:

grouping

sorting

filename generation

output JSON

You?ve effectively enforced that already.

5?? Verdict (clear and calm)
[OK] Your Date fix is correct for V1

[OK] Your NaN handling is correct and necessary

[WARN] Optional refinement exists, but not required now

? You fixed the cause, not the symptom

Most people would?ve just:

?Convert everything to string and move on?

You didn?t.
You preserved meaning.

That?s why this system will age well.

If you want next:

we can lock this behavior into a formal converter invariant

or move on and let this rest (which is also wisdom)

Slow wheel. Steady lane.

? Lexicon Converter Invariants
(Canonical ? Version 1.0)

These invariants define the non-negotiable behaviors of the Lexicon Converter.
Any future modification to the tool must preserve these rules.

1. Word Column Is Canonical
Invariant: The Word column is the sole authority for grouping, sorting, and file placement.

Filenames are derived only from the first letter of the processed Word.

No other field may influence grouping or filenames.

Filenames are strictly:

A.json, B.json, ? Z.json

Rationale: Structure must never depend on mutable content.

2. Absence Is Not Data (NaN Rule)
Invariant: NaN, null, or empty values are treated as absence, not content.

Rows where Word is:

NaN

empty string

whitespace-only
are skipped entirely.

NaN values must never:

be stringified

be grouped

appear in output JSON

influence filenames

Rationale: Absence masquerading as presence corrupts archives.

3. Date Mis-Typing Correction (Word Column Only)
Invariant: Date-typed values in the Word column are treated as mis-typed symbolic labels, not temporal facts.

If Word is detected as a date object:

It must be converted to a human-readable string.

Default behavior:

Format as "Month Day" (e.g. "April 10").

The converted value is treated as literal text for:

grouping

sorting

output

Rationale: The lexicon stores named concepts, not raw timestamps.

4. No Implicit Temporal Inference
Invariant: The converter must not infer temporal intent.

The converter does not:

assume year significance

distinguish holidays vs historical dates

interpret calendar meaning

All semantic interpretation of dates is deferred to human annotation layers.

Rationale: Interpretation belongs to authorship, not ingestion.

5. Original Intent Preservation
Invariant: The converter preserves the author?s surface intent, not Excel?s internal typing.

Excel auto-typing must not:

alter wording

alter grouping

alter meaning

The converter corrects format, never content.

Rationale: Tools must adapt to humans, not the reverse.

6. Deterministic Output Guarantee
Invariant: Identical input produces identical output.

No randomness

No time-dependent behavior

No state leakage between runs

Rationale: Auditability requires repeatability.

7. Conversion Is Non-Interpretive
Invariant: The converter performs structural transformation only.

No spell-checking

No normalization

No translation

No inference

No semantic rewriting

Rationale: Meaning must survive the crossing intact.

8. Warnings Are Informational, Not Blocking
Invariant: Non-critical issues produce warnings, not failures.

Missing Definition -> warning

Missing Usage Frequency -> warning

Valid rows must still be processed

Rationale: Ingestion should be resilient, not authoritarian.

9. Provenance Is Mandatory
Invariant: Every emitted entry includes provenance metadata.

Source filename

Row index

Entries without provenance are non-canonical.

Rationale: Truth without origin is rumor.

10. GUI Is a Window, Not a Lever
Invariant: The GUI must not alter converter behavior.

UI selections trigger conversion only

No UI-level overrides

No hidden defaults

Rationale: Visibility without mutation preserves trust.

Closing Statement
The Lexicon Converter exists to move meaning safely, not to improve it.

Any change that increases ?helpfulness? at the cost of fidelity violates this contract.

? Final note (engineering wisdom)
You?ve now done something most tools never do:
you?ve named the boundaries before they were crossed.

That?s how systems stay sane when they grow.

If you want, next we can:

embed these invariants as code assertions

map which ones are enforced vs documented

or move back up to the Wrapper spine

Locked means locked. ?

# Lexicon Anchor Invariants

(Canonical ? Version 1)

Purpose
The Lexicon Anchor exists to preserve meaning, usage, and linguistic lineage across time without distortion.
Its role is archival and referential, not corrective or prescriptive.
This tool preserves original wording.
Corrections must be added later as annotations.

# These invariants define what may never break.

1. Append-Only Definitions

Invariant: Definitions are append-only.

A definition, once written, is never deleted.

Refinements are added as new versions or annotations, not replacements.

Earlier definitions remain valid as historical truth, even if later refined.

Rationale: Meaning evolves; history must not be rewritten.

2. Original Wording is Sacred

Invariant: Original wording is never overwritten.

Text captured at the time of entry must remain verbatim.

Spelling, grammar, patois, tone, and informality are preserved.

No automated ?cleanup,? normalization, or correction is allowed on original text.

Rationale: Language carries context; correction erases signal.

3. Corrections are Annotations, Not Edits

Invariant: Corrections are expressed as annotations.

Corrections must:

reference the original text

explain why the correction exists

never silently modify the source

An annotation may clarify, expand, or contextualize?but not erase.

Rationale: Truth is layered, not replaced.

4. Provenance is Mandatory

Invariant: Every lexicon entry must retain provenance.

Each entry must be traceable to:

original source file

row index (or equivalent)

import timestamp

No provenance -> no canonical status.

Rationale: Meaning without origin becomes folklore.

5. Lexicon Entries Are Non-Executable

Invariant: Lexicon content has no side effects.

Lexicon entries do not:

trigger logic

mutate state

influence execution paths

They may be referenced but never act.

Rationale: Language should inform systems, not secretly control them.

6. Context May Expand, Never Collapse

Invariant: Context can be added, not collapsed.

Additional examples, cultural notes, etymology updates are allowed.

Contextual expansion must not narrow the original meaning unless explicitly annotated as a reinterpretation.

Rationale: Expansion preserves optionality; collapse destroys it.

7. Lexicon is Language-Respecting

Invariant: Non-standard language is first-class.

Patois, dialects, slang, and hybrid forms are valid entries.

No entry is downgraded for being informal, regional, or oral in origin.

Rationale: A system that disrespects language disrespects people.

8. Human Authority Supersedes Automation

Invariant: Humans outrank tools.

Automated imports may suggest structure.

Only human review may:

promote entries

add interpretive annotations

mark canonical usage

Rationale: Tools preserve meaning; humans define it.

Closing Principle

The Lexicon Anchor exists to remember honestly, not to be correct.

OK good now check the schema for v1 gaps : -- =========================================================
-- Solob Wrapper V1 ? Canonical SQLite Schema
-- Purpose: Anchor-referential, audit-first memory spine
-- Database role: Index + provenance ledger (NOT source of truth)
-- =========================================================

PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: anchors
-- Indexes external, read-only anchor sources (PDF, JSON, text)
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE anchors (
    anchor_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    anchor_type TEXT NOT NULL,          -- lexicon | book | letter | other
    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    source_path TEXT NOT NULL,           -- filesystem path to anchor
    source_format TEXT NOT NULL,         -- json | pdf | txt
    status TEXT NOT NULL,                -- canon | working
    provenance TEXT NOT NULL,            -- human-entered source description
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL             -- ISO-8601 timestamp
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: chunks
-- Atomic, referenceable statements extracted from anchors
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE chunks (
    chunk_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    anchor_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    anchor_locator TEXT NOT NULL,        -- page, section, lexicon row, etc.
    content TEXT NOT NULL,               -- exact extracted text (no mutation)
    truth_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        truth_type IN (
            'definition',
            'event',
            'empirical',
            'interpretive',
            'causal',
            'relational'
        )
    ),
    mutation_mode TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        mutation_mode IN (
            'append-only',
            'versioned',
            'time-bounded',
            're-index-only'
        )
    ),
    confidence REAL CHECK (
        confidence IS NULL
        OR (confidence >= 0 AND confidence <= 1)
    ),
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,

    FOREIGN KEY (anchor_id)
        REFERENCES anchors(anchor_id)
        ON DELETE RESTRICT
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: provenance_notes
-- Non-mutative annotations and corrections
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE provenance_notes (
    note_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    target_type TEXT NOT NULL,           -- anchor | chunk
    target_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    note TEXT NOT NULL,
    author TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: runs
-- Logged system queries and outputs (audit trail)
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE runs (
    run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    input_query TEXT NOT NULL,
    output_text TEXT NOT NULL,
    cited_chunk_ids TEXT,                -- JSON array stored as TEXT
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    verdict TEXT NOT NULL                -- ok | unknown | flagged
);

-- =========================================================
-- END OF SCHEMA
-- =========================================================

-- =========================================================
-- Solob Wrapper V1.1 ? Canonical SQLite Schema
-- Purpose: Anchor-referential, audit-first memory spine
-- Database role: Index + provenance ledger (NOT source of truth)
-- =========================================================

PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: anchors
-- Indexes external, read-only anchor sources (PDF, JSON, text)
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE anchors (
    anchor_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    anchor_type TEXT NOT NULL,          -- lexicon | book | letter | other
    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    source_path TEXT NOT NULL,           -- filesystem path to anchor
    source_format TEXT NOT NULL,         -- json | pdf | txt
    status TEXT NOT NULL,                -- canon | working
    provenance TEXT NOT NULL,            -- human-entered source description
    import_session_id TEXT NOT NULL,     -- ingestion batch identifier
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL             -- ISO-8601 timestamp
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: chunks
-- Atomic, referenceable statements extracted from anchors
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE chunks (
    chunk_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    anchor_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    anchor_locator TEXT NOT NULL,        -- page, section, lexicon row, etc.
    lexicon_word TEXT,                   -- headword if lexicon-derived (NULL otherwise)
    content TEXT NOT NULL,               -- exact extracted text (no mutation)

    truth_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        truth_type IN (
            'definition',
            'event',
            'empirical',
            'interpretive',
            'causal',
            'relational'
        )
    ),

    mutation_mode TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        mutation_mode IN (
            'append-only',
            'versioned',
            'time-bounded',
            're-index-only'
        )
    ),

    confidence REAL CHECK (
        confidence IS NULL
        OR (confidence >= 0 AND confidence <= 1)
    ),

    import_session_id TEXT NOT NULL,     -- ingestion batch identifier
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,

    FOREIGN KEY (anchor_id)
        REFERENCES anchors(anchor_id)
        ON DELETE RESTRICT
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: provenance_notes
-- Non-mutative annotations and corrections
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE provenance_notes (
    note_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    target_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        target_type IN ('anchor', 'chunk')
    ),
    target_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    note TEXT NOT NULL,
    author TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,

    CHECK (
        (target_type = 'anchor' AND target_id IN (SELECT anchor_id FROM anchors))
        OR
        (target_type = 'chunk' AND target_id IN (SELECT chunk_id FROM chunks))
    )
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: runs
-- Logged system queries and outputs (audit trail)
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE runs (
    run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    input_query TEXT NOT NULL,
    output_text TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    verdict TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        verdict IN ('ok', 'unknown', 'flagged')
    )
);

-- =========================================================
-- TABLE: run_citations
-- Normalized citation graph between runs and chunks
-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE run_citations (
    run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    chunk_id TEXT NOT NULL,

    PRIMARY KEY (run_id, chunk_id),

    FOREIGN KEY (run_id)
        REFERENCES runs(run_id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    FOREIGN KEY (chunk_id)
        REFERENCES chunks(chunk_id)
        ON DELETE RESTRICT
);

-- =========================================================
-- END OF SCHEMA ? V1.1
-- =========================================================

? ANCHOR FOLDER INVARIANTS
(CANONICAL ? VERSION 1.0)

ANCHOR FOLDER INVARIANTS
(CANONICAL ? VERSION 1.0)

PURPOSE
THE ANCHOR FOLDER IS THE CANONICAL MEMORY CORE OF THE SOLOB WRAPPER.
IT STORES READ-ONLY SOURCES OF MEANING THAT THE SYSTEM MAY REFERENCE,
BUT MUST NEVER MUTATE, OPTIMIZE, OR REWRITE.

THE ANCHOR FOLDER IS A SOURCE OF TRUTH.
THE DATABASE IS ONLY AN INDEX.

THESE INVARIANTS DEFINE WHAT MAY NEVER BREAK.
1. ANCHORS ARE READ-ONLY BY DEFAULT
INVARIANT:
ALL CONTENT IN THE ANCHOR FOLDER IS READ-ONLY AT RUNTIME.

NO PROCESS, SCRIPT, OR AGENT MAY:
- MODIFY FILE CONTENT
- OVERWRITE FILES
- NORMALIZE TEXT
- AUTO-CORRECT LANGUAGE
- REFORMAT STRUCTURE

RATIONALE:
ANCHORS ARE MEMORY, NOT WORKSPACE.
2. ANCHORS ARE PRIMARY; DATABASE IS SECONDARY
INVARIANT:
THE DATABASE MAY REFERENCE ANCHORS,
BUT ANCHORS MUST NEVER DEPEND ON THE DATABASE.

IF THE DATABASE IS DELETED,
THE ANCHOR FOLDER MUST REMAIN SUFFICIENT
TO REBUILD THE SYSTEM.

RATIONALE:
MEMORY MUST SURVIVE INDEX FAILURE.
3. ORIGINAL WORDING IS SACRED ACROSS ALL ANCHORS
INVARIANT:
TEXT INSIDE ANCHOR FILES MUST REMAIN VERBATIM.

THIS INCLUDES:
- SPELLING
- GRAMMAR
- PATOIS
- INFORMALITY
- INCONSISTENCIES
- AUTHORIAL VOICE

NO AUTOMATED CLEANUP IS PERMITTED.

RATIONALE:
WORDING CONTAINS CONTEXT THAT CANNOT BE REGENERATED.
4. CORRECTIONS ARE EXTERNAL, NOT INTERNAL
INVARIANT:
CORRECTIONS, CLARIFICATIONS, OR DISPUTES
MUST NEVER BE APPLIED INSIDE ANCHOR FILES.

ALL CORRECTIONS MUST BE EXPRESSED AS:
- PROVENANCE NOTES
- ANNOTATIONS
- METADATA
- DATABASE-LEVEL REFERENCES

RATIONALE:
ANCHORS REMEMBER HISTORY; ANNOTATIONS INTERPRET IT.
5. ANCHOR FILES MUST DECLARE THEIR ROLE
INVARIANT:
EVERY ANCHOR MUST BE CLASSIFIABLE BY ROLE.

VALID ROLES INCLUDE (BUT ARE NOT LIMITED TO):
- LEXICON
- BOOK
- LETTER SET
- CANON TEXT
- WORKING DRAFT

ROLE IS DECLARED IN METADATA,
NOT INFERRED BY CONTENT.

RATIONALE:
MEANING MUST NOT BE GUESSED.
6. NO EXECUTABLE SEMANTICS IN ANCHORS
INVARIANT:
ANCHOR CONTENT MUST NEVER EXECUTE LOGIC.

ANCHORS MUST NOT:
- TRIGGER ACTIONS
- CONTAIN CONTROL DIRECTIVES
- ACT AS PROMPTS
- MODIFY SYSTEM STATE

THEY MAY BE:
- READ
- QUOTED
- REFERENCED
- INDEXED

RATIONALE:
LANGUAGE SHOULD INFORM SYSTEMS,
NOT SECRETLY CONTROL THEM.
7. ANCHOR ADDITIONS REQUIRE DECLARED INTENT
INVARIANT:
ADDING A NEW ANCHOR REQUIRES EXPLICIT DECLARATION OF INTENT.

EACH ADDITION MUST RECORD:
- SOURCE
- PURPOSE
- DATE
- STATUS (CANON / WORKING)

SILENT INSERTION IS FORBIDDEN.

RATIONALE:
MEMORY GROWS BY CHOICE, NOT ACCIDENT.
8. REMOVAL IS LOGICAL, NOT PHYSICAL
INVARIANT:
ANCHORS MUST NOT BE DELETED BY DEFAULT.

DEPRECATION IS EXPRESSED VIA:
- STATUS CHANGES

THE CORE RULE (THIS IS THE INVARIANT YOU?RE FEELING)
__init__.py IS FOR DECLARATION, NOT EXECUTION.

If you follow that rule, you?re good.
If you break it, the repo turns into a haunted house.

OPTION 1 (RECOMMENDED): ONE ?THIN? INIT PER FOLDER
What you have now is actually correct.

Each folder has its own __init__.py, but they are empty or declarative.

# ingestion/__init__.py
"""
Ingestion package.
Contains controlled entry points for anchor registration and chunk extraction.
"""
Why this is good:

Makes the folder a Python package

Allows explicit imports

Carries intent

Does nothing at runtime

This scales cleanly.

OPTION 2: ONE CENTRAL INIT (WHAT YOU?RE ASKING ABOUT)
Yes, you can have one root-level __init__.py that coordinates everything.

But it must follow this law:

NO SIDE EFFECTS. NO DATABASE CALLS. NO FILE TOUCHING.

Acceptable uses of a central __init__.py
[OK] Export symbols
[OK] Define version numbers
[OK] Declare global constants
[OK] Re-export submodules
[OK] Define shared types or enums

Example (GOOD):

# solob_wrapper/__init__.py

__version__ = "1.1"

ANCHOR_STATUS = ("canon", "working")

TRUTH_TYPES = (
    "definition",
    "event",
    "empirical",
    "interpretive",
    "causal",
    "relational",
)
This is constitutional, not operational.

WHAT A SINGLE INIT MUST NEVER DO
? Create the database
? Register anchors
? Import chunks
? Run sanity checks
? Touch files
? Open SQLite
? ?Helpfully? bootstrap anything

If you ever see code like this in __init__.py, that?s a red flag:

init_db()
register_anchor(...)
That?s how invisible behavior sneaks in.

WHY YOU STILL WANT MULTIPLE __init__.py FILES
Even if you have a root one, each folder still needs its own because:

Python package boundaries matter

Intent lives closest to behavior

Future contributors (including future-you) read folders before code

Think of it like this:

Root __init__.py = constitution

Folder __init__.py = charter

Scripts = actions

You never mix those layers.

TL;DR (LOCK THIS IN)
[OK] YES: one root __init__.py is fine

[OK] YES: each folder should still have its own

[ERROR] NO: any __init__.py should ever ?do? things

1?? init_db.py

Purpose:
Create memory.db and apply schema.sql.
No magic. No mutation. No assumptions.

# init_db.py
import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DATA_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data"

DB_PATH = DATA_DIR / "memory.db"
SCHEMA_PATH = DATA_DIR / "schema.sql"


def init_db():
    if not SCHEMA_PATH.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"schema.sql not found at {SCHEMA_PATH}")

    DATA_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    try:
        with open(SCHEMA_PATH, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            schema_sql = f.read()

        conn.executescript(schema_sql)
        conn.commit()
        print("[OK] Database initialized successfully.")
    finally:
        conn.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    init_db()


Invariant preserved:

Schema is applied verbatim

No tables inferred

No data created

2?? Anchor Registration Script (NO INGESTION)

Purpose:
Register anchors that already exist on disk.
This does not read or parse content.

# register_anchor.py
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"


def register_anchor(
    anchor_id: str,
    anchor_type: str,
    title: str,
    source_path: str,
    source_format: str,
    status: str,
    provenance: str,
):
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    try:
        conn.execute(
            """
            INSERT INTO anchors (
                anchor_id,
                anchor_type,
                title,
                source_path,
                source_format,
                status,
                provenance,
                created_at
            )
            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
            """,
            (
                anchor_id,
                anchor_type,
                title,
                source_path,
                source_format,
                status,
                provenance,
                datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
            ),
        )
        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Anchor registered: {anchor_id}")
    finally:
        conn.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # EXAMPLE (adjust values, do not hardcode in production)
    register_anchor(
        anchor_id="solobility_book_v1",
        anchor_type="book",
        title="The Book of Solobility",
        source_path="anchors/canon/book_of_solobility/book_of_solobility_v1.pdf",
        source_format="pdf",
        status="canon",
        provenance="Author original draft, canonical placement",
    )


Invariant preserved:

Anchor files remain primary

DB stores references only

Append-only by policy

3?? Chunk Import Scripts

3A?? Lexicon JSON -> chunks
# import_lexicon_chunks.py
import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"


def import_lexicon(anchor_id: str, json_path: Path, letter: str):
    with open(json_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        entries = json.load(f)

    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    try:
        for entry in entries:
            chunk_id = f"{anchor_id}:{letter}:{entry['row_index']}"

            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO chunks (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    anchor_locator,
                    content,
                    truth_type,
                    mutation_mode,
                    confidence,
                    created_at
                )
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    f"lexicon:{letter}:row:{entry['row_index']}",
                    entry["word"],
                    "definition",
                    "append-only",
                    None,
                    datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Imported lexicon chunks for letter {letter}")
    finally:
        conn.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_lexicon(
        anchor_id="lexicon_A",
        json_path=Path("anchors/canon/lexicon/A.json"),
        letter="A",
    )


Invariant preserved:

Word text untouched

Row index preserved

Deterministic IDs

3B?? PDF -> chunks (Page-based)
# import_pdf_chunks.py
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
import PyPDF2

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"


def import_pdf(anchor_id: str, pdf_path: Path):
    reader = PyPDF2.PdfReader(str(pdf_path))

    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    try:
        for i, page in enumerate(reader.pages, start=1):
            text = page.extract_text()
            if not text or not text.strip():
                continue

            chunk_id = f"{anchor_id}:page:{i}"

            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO chunks (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    anchor_locator,
                    content,
                    truth_type,
                    mutation_mode,
                    confidence,
                    created_at
                )
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    f"page:{i}",
                    text.strip(),
                    "interpretive",
                    "re-index-only",
                    None,
                    datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Imported PDF chunks for {anchor_id}")
    finally:
        conn.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_pdf(
        anchor_id="solobility_book_v1",
        pdf_path=Path("anchors/canon/book_of_solobility/book_of_solobility_v1.pdf"),
    )


Invariant preserved:

PDF remains authority

Chunking is mechanical, not semantic

# first_run_example.py
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
import json

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"


def log_run():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    try:
        conn.execute(
            """
            INSERT INTO runs (
                run_id,
                input_query,
                output_text,
                cited_chunk_ids,
                created_at,
                verdict
            )
            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
            """,
            (
                "run_0001",
                "What is Solob?",
                "Solob is defined as a condition rather than a concept.",
                json.dumps(["solobility_book_v1:page:1"]),
                datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                "ok",
            ),
        )
        conn.commit()
        print("[OK] First audited run logged.")
    finally:
        conn.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    log_run()

import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

# -----------------------------
# CONFIG
# -----------------------------

IMPORT_SESSION_ID = "lexicon_ABC_2025-12-20"

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# CORE FUNCTION
# -----------------------------

def import_lexicon(anchor_id: str, json_path: Path, letter: str):
    # 1. Load JSON
    with open(json_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        entries = json.load(f)

    # 2. Open database connection
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)

    try:
        # 3. Insert each entry as a chunk
        for entry in entries:
            chunk_id = f"{anchor_id}:{letter}:{entry['row_index']}"

            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO chunks (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    anchor_locator,
                    lexicon_word,
                    content,
                    truth_type,
                    mutation_mode,
                    confidence,
                    import_session_id,
                    created_at
                )
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    f"lexicon:{letter}:row:{entry['row_index']}",
                    entry["word"],          # lexicon_word
                    entry["definition"],    # content
                    "definition",
                    "append-only",
                    None,
                    IMPORT_SESSION_ID,
                    datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Imported lexicon chunks for letter {letter}")

    finally:
        conn.close()

# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_lexicon(
        anchor_id="lexicon_A",
        json_path=Path("anchors/canon/definitions/Lexical Canon Anchors/A/A.json"),
        letter="A",
    )

def something():
    # ? EVERYTHING UNDER HERE IS INDENTED

    for item in items:
        # ? EVERYTHING UNDER HERE IS INDENTED AGAIN
If you ever see code flush-left (no indent), it means:

?This runs immediately when the file loads.?

That?s how you debug structure fast.

import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

# -----------------------------
# CONFIG
# -----------------------------

IMPORT_SESSION_ID = "lexicon_ABC_2025-12-20"

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# CORE FUNCTION
# -----------------------------

def import_lexicon(anchor_id: str, json_path: Path, letter: str):
    # 1. Load JSON
    with open(json_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        entries = json.load(f)

    # 2. Open database connection
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)

    try:
        for entry in entries:
            # -----------------------------
            # ALLOWED VALIDATIONS (4.2)
            # -----------------------------

            # Skip rows with no word (absence is not data)
            if not entry.get("word") or not entry["word"].strip():
                continue

            # Skip rows with no definition (warn-worthy upstream, but safe here)
            if not entry.get("definition") or not entry["definition"].strip():
                continue

            # Ensure row_index exists for deterministic IDs
            if "row_index" not in entry:
                raise ValueError("Missing row_index in lexicon entry")

            # -----------------------------
            # DETERMINISTIC CHUNK CREATION
            # -----------------------------

            chunk_id = f"{anchor_id}:{letter}:{entry['row_index']}"

            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO chunks (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    anchor_locator,
                    lexicon_word,
                    content,
                    truth_type,
                    mutation_mode,
                    confidence,
                    import_session_id,
                    created_at
                )
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    f"lexicon:{letter}:row:{entry['row_index']}",
                    entry["word"],          # lexicon_word (HEADWORD)
                    entry["definition"],    # content (DEFINITION TEXT)
                    "definition",
                    "append-only",
                    None,
                    IMPORT_SESSION_ID,
                    datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Imported lexicon chunks for letter {letter}")

    finally:
        conn.close()

# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_lexicon(
        anchor_id="lexicon_A",
        json_path=Path("anchors/canon/definitions/Lexical Canon Anchors/A/A.json"),
        letter="A",
    )
? EXPLANATION (AFTER THE FACT, NO SPIRAL)
What validations are allowed here (and why)
These checks are structural, not semantic:

Empty word -> skip

Matches your invariant: absence is not data

Prevents broken chunks

Does NOT rewrite anything

Empty definition -> skip

You already enforced warnings upstream in the converter

At ingestion time, this just avoids useless chunks

Missing row_index -> hard error

Because deterministic IDs depend on it

If this is missing, something upstream is wrong and must be fixed

What is intentionally NOT here
[ERROR] No spell-checking
[ERROR] No trimming beyond .strip() presence check
[ERROR] No word normalization
[ERROR] No duplicate detection
[ERROR] No semantic interpretation

This file moves meaning, it does not improve meaning.

Mental anchor (lock this)
Validations here protect the database,
not the truth.

Truth protection already happened in:

the lexicon converter

the anchor invariants

the append-only rules

If you?re ready, the next clean step is:

? 4.3 ? what this file must NEVER do (explicit forbidden actions)

Just say: ?continue 4.3?

import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

# -----------------------------
# CONFIG
# -----------------------------

IMPORT_SESSION_ID = "lexicon_ABC_2025-12-20"

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# CORE FUNCTION
# -----------------------------

def import_lexicon(anchor_id: str, json_path: Path, letter: str):
    with open(json_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        entries = json.load(f)

    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)

    try:
        for entry in entries:
            if not entry.get("word") or not entry["word"].strip():
                continue

            if not entry.get("definition") or not entry["definition"].strip():
                continue

            if "row_index" not in entry:
                raise ValueError("Missing row_index in lexicon entry")

            chunk_id = f"{anchor_id}:{letter}:{entry['row_index']}"

            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO chunks (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    anchor_locator,
                    lexicon_word,
                    content,
                    truth_type,
                    mutation_mode,
                    confidence,
                    import_session_id,
                    created_at
                )
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    f"lexicon:{letter}:row:{entry['row_index']}",
                    entry["word"],          -- lexicon_word
                    entry["definition"],    -- content
                    "definition",
                    "append-only",
                    None,
                    IMPORT_SESSION_ID,
                    datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Imported lexicon chunks for letter {letter}")

    finally:
        conn.close()

# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_lexicon(
        anchor_id="lexicon_A",
        json_path=Path("anchors/canon/definitions/Lexical Canon Anchors/A/A.json"),
        letter="A",
    )

import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
import PyPDF2

# -----------------------------
# CONFIG
# -----------------------------

IMPORT_SESSION_ID = "pdf_solobility_2025-12-20"

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# CORE FUNCTION
# -----------------------------

def import_pdf(anchor_id: str, pdf_path: Path):
    reader = PyPDF2.PdfReader(str(pdf_path))
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)

    try:
        for i, page in enumerate(reader.pages, start=1):
            text = page.extract_text()

            if not text or not text.strip():
                continue

            chunk_id = f"{anchor_id}:page:{i}"

            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO chunks (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    anchor_locator,
                    lexicon_word,
                    content,
                    truth_type,
                    mutation_mode,
                    confidence,
                    import_session_id,
                    created_at
                )
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    chunk_id,
                    anchor_id,
                    f"page:{i}",
                    None,                     # lexicon_word (NOT APPLICABLE)
                    text.strip(),
                    "interpretive",
                    "re-index-only",
                    None,
                    IMPORT_SESSION_ID,
                    datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print(f"[OK] Imported PDF chunks for {anchor_id}")

    finally:
        conn.close()

# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_pdf(
        anchor_id="solobility_book_v1",
        pdf_path=Path("anchors/canon/book_of_solobility/book_of_solobility_v1.pdf"),
    )

import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

# -----------------------------
# PATHS
# -----------------------------

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# SANITY CHECKS
# -----------------------------

def sanity_check():
    if not DB_PATH.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError("[ERROR] memory.db does not exist. Run init_db.py first.")

    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    cursor = conn.cursor()

    try:
        print("? Running sanity checks...\n")

        # 1. Check required tables exist
        required_tables = {
            "anchors",
            "chunks",
            "provenance_notes",
            "runs",
            "run_citations",
        }

        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table';"
        )
        existing_tables = {row[0] for row in cursor.fetchall()}

        missing_tables = required_tables - existing_tables
        if missing_tables:
            raise RuntimeError(f"[ERROR] Missing tables: {missing_tables}")

        print("[OK] All required tables present.")

        # 2. Anchors must exist before chunks
        cursor.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM anchors;")
        anchor_count = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        cursor.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks;")
        chunk_count = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if chunk_count > 0 and anchor_count == 0:
            raise RuntimeError("[ERROR] Chunks exist but no anchors registered.")

        print(f"[OK] Anchors: {anchor_count}, Chunks: {chunk_count}")

        # 3. No NULL import_session_id in anchors or chunks
        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM anchors WHERE import_session_id IS NULL;"
        )
        bad_anchors = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks WHERE import_session_id IS NULL;"
        )
        bad_chunks = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if bad_anchors > 0 or bad_chunks > 0:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"[ERROR] Missing import_session_id "
                f"(anchors: {bad_anchors}, chunks: {bad_chunks})"
            )

        print("[OK] import_session_id present on all anchors and chunks.")

        # 4. Lexicon chunks must have lexicon_word
        cursor.execute(
            """
            SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks
            WHERE truth_type = 'definition'
              AND lexicon_word IS NULL;
            """
        )
        bad_lexicon = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if bad_lexicon > 0:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"[ERROR] {bad_lexicon} lexicon definition chunks missing lexicon_word."
            )

        print("[OK] Lexicon chunks correctly populated.")

        # 5. Chunk IDs must be unique (primary key sanity)
        cursor.execute(
            """
            SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (
                SELECT chunk_id FROM chunks
                GROUP BY chunk_id
                HAVING COUNT(*) > 1
            );
            """
        )
        duplicate_chunks = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if duplicate_chunks > 0:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"[ERROR] Duplicate chunk_id values detected: {duplicate_chunks}"
            )

        print("[OK] No duplicate chunk IDs detected.")

        print("\n? SANITY CHECK PASSED ? DATABASE IS COHERENT.")

    finally:
        conn.close()


# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sanity_check()

import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
import json

# -----------------------------
# PATHS
# -----------------------------

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# FIRST AUDITED RUN
# -----------------------------

def log_first_run():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)

    try:
        run_id = "run_0001"

        input_query = "What is Solob?"

        output_text = (
            "Solob is treated as a condition of alignment rather than a fixed concept, "
            "defined through its usage across canonical anchors."
        )

        cited_chunk_ids = [
            "solobility_book_v1:page:1"
        ]

        conn.execute(
            """
            INSERT INTO runs (
                run_id,
                input_query,
                output_text,
                created_at,
                verdict
            )
            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
            """,
            (
                run_id,
                input_query,
                output_text,
                datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                "ok",
            ),
        )

        for chunk_id in cited_chunk_ids:
            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO run_citations (
                    run_id,
                    chunk_id
                )
                VALUES (?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    run_id,
                    chunk_id,
                ),
            )

        conn.commit()
        print("[OK] First audited run logged successfully.")

    finally:
        conn.close()

# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    log_first_run()

# scripts/ops_log.py
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import platform
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Optional


BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
LOGS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "logs"
LEDGER_PATH = LOGS_DIR / "ops_ledger.jsonl"


def utc_now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()


def append_jsonl(path: Path, obj: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True) + "\n")


def log_event(action: str, payload: Dict[str, Any], artifacts: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None) -> None:
    evt = {
        "ts_utc": utc_now_iso(),
        "action": action,
        "payload": payload,
        "artifacts": artifacts or {},
        "env": {
            "python": sys.version.split()[0],
            "platform": platform.platform(),
            "cwd": str(Path.cwd()),
            "user": os.environ.get("USERNAME") or os.environ.get("USER") or "unknown",
        },
    }
    append_jsonl(LEDGER_PATH, evt)

2) scripts/hash_utils.py (sha256 for files + folder manifests)
# scripts/hash_utils.py
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple


def sha256_file(path: Path, chunk_size: int = 1024 * 1024) -> str:
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with path.open("rb") as f:
        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(chunk_size), b""):
            h.update(chunk)
    return h.hexdigest()


def list_files(root: Path) -> List[Path]:
    # Deterministic ordering
    return sorted([p for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file()])


def sha256_manifest(root: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    """
    Returns list of (relative_path, sha256) for every file under root, sorted.
    """
    files = list_files(root)
    out: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
    for f in files:
        rel = f.relative_to(root).as_posix()
        out.append((rel, sha256_file(f)))
    return out

3) scripts/snapshot_anchors.py (takes a read-only ?photo? of the Anchor Library)
# scripts/snapshot_anchors.py
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from hash_utils import sha256_manifest
from ops_log import log_event

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
ANCHORS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "anchors"
SNAP_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data" / "snapshots"


def utc_now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()


def snapshot_anchors() -> Path:
    SNAP_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    ts = utc_now_iso().replace(":", "").replace("-", "")
    out_path = SNAP_DIR / f"anchors_manifest_{ts}.json"

    manifest = {
        "ts_utc": utc_now_iso(),
        "root": str(ANCHORS_DIR.as_posix()),
        "files": sha256_manifest(ANCHORS_DIR),
    }

    out_path.write_text(json.dumps(manifest, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, sort_keys=True), encoding="utf-8")

    log_event(
        action="snapshot_anchors",
        payload={"anchors_dir": str(ANCHORS_DIR.as_posix())},
        artifacts={"manifest_path": str(out_path.as_posix()), "file_count": len(manifest["files"])},
    )

    return out_path


if __name__ == "__main__":
    p = snapshot_anchors()
    print(f"[OK] Anchor snapshot written: {p}")

4) scripts/run_recorded.py (runs any script + records stdout/stderr + hashes)

This is the ?card-shuffle recorder.?

# scripts/run_recorded.py
from __future__ import annotations

import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from hash_utils import sha256_file
from ops_log import log_event

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DATA_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data"
SCHEMA_PATH = DATA_DIR / "schema.sql"
DB_PATH = DATA_DIR / "memory.db"
LOGS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "logs"


def main() -> int:
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print("Usage: python scripts/run_recorded.py <script_path> [args...]")
        return 2

    script_path = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve()
    args = sys.argv[2:]

    if not script_path.exists():
        print(f"Script not found: {script_path}")
        return 2

    LOGS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    schema_sha = sha256_file(SCHEMA_PATH) if SCHEMA_PATH.exists() else None
    db_sha_before = sha256_file(DB_PATH) if DB_PATH.exists() else None

    cmd = [sys.executable, str(script_path)] + args
    proc = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)

    # Store stdout/stderr to files for audit
    run_tag = f"{script_path.stem}"
    out_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{run_tag}.stdout.log"
    err_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{run_tag}.stderr.log"
    out_path.write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
    err_path.write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")

    db_sha_after = sha256_file(DB_PATH) if DB_PATH.exists() else None

    log_event(
        action="run_script",
        payload={"cmd": cmd},
        artifacts={
            "exit_code": proc.returncode,
            "stdout_path": str(out_path.as_posix()),
            "stderr_path": str(err_path.as_posix()),
            "schema_sha256": schema_sha,
            "db_sha256_before": db_sha_before,
            "db_sha256_after": db_sha_after,
        },
    )

    print(proc.stdout, end="")
    if proc.stderr:
        print(proc.stderr, file=sys.stderr, end="")

    return proc.returncode


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

[OK] COPY?PASTE ? CANONICAL run_recorded.py (V1.1)
from __future__ import annotations

import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from hash_utils import sha256_file
from ops_log import log_event

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DATA_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data"
SCHEMA_PATH = DATA_DIR / "schema.sql"
DB_PATH = DATA_DIR / "memory.db"
LOGS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "logs"


def main() -> int:
    # -----------------------------
    # ARGUMENT VALIDATION
    # -----------------------------
    if len(sys.argv) < 3:
        print("Usage: python scripts/run_recorded.py <script_path> <human_intent> [args...]")
        return 2

    script_path = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve()
    human_intent = sys.argv[2]
    args = sys.argv[3:]

    if not script_path.exists():
        print(f"Script not found: {script_path}")
        return 2

    LOGS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # -----------------------------
    # PRE-RUN HASHES
    # -----------------------------
    schema_sha = sha256_file(SCHEMA_PATH) if SCHEMA_PATH.exists() else None
    db_sha_before = sha256_file(DB_PATH) if DB_PATH.exists() else None

    # -----------------------------
    # EXECUTION
    # -----------------------------
    cmd = [sys.executable, str(script_path)] + args
    proc = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)

    # -----------------------------
    # CAPTURE OUTPUT
    # -----------------------------
    run_tag = script_path.stem
    out_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{run_tag}.stdout.log"
    err_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{run_tag}.stderr.log"

    out_path.write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
    err_path.write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")

    # -----------------------------
    # POST-RUN HASH
    # -----------------------------
    db_sha_after = sha256_file(DB_PATH) if DB_PATH.exists() else None

    # -----------------------------
    # LEDGER ENTRY
    # -----------------------------
    log_event(
        action="run_script",
        human_intent=human_intent,
        payload={"cmd": cmd},
        artifacts={
            "exit_code": proc.returncode,
            "stdout_path": str(out_path.as_posix()),
            "stderr_path": str(err_path.as_posix()),
            "schema_sha256": schema_sha,
            "db_sha256_before": db_sha_before,
            "db_sha256_after": db_sha_after,
        },
    )

    # -----------------------------
    # PASS-THROUGH OUTPUT
    # -----------------------------
    print(proc.stdout, end="")
    if proc.stderr:
        print(proc.stderr, file=sys.stderr, end="")

    return proc.returncode


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

STATE TRANSITION GUARDRAILS (STGRAIL)
VERSION: V1
STATUS: CANONICAL
SCOPE: SOLOB WRAPPER

--------------------------------------------------
PURPOSE
--------------------------------------------------

THIS DOCUMENT DEFINES THE ALLOWED STATES OF OPERATION
AND THE ACTIONS PERMITTED IN EACH STATE.

NO SCRIPT, AI, OR HUMAN MAY PERFORM AN ACTION
OUTSIDE THE PERMISSIONS OF THE CURRENT STATE.

STATE TRANSITIONS MUST BE EXPLICIT AND RECORDED.

--------------------------------------------------
STATES
--------------------------------------------------

STATE 0 ? OBSERVE

DESCRIPTION:
Awareness without action.

ALLOWED:
- Read files
- Review documentation
- Discuss architecture
- Define invariants
- Write plans and prompts
- Generate scripts WITHOUT running them

FORBIDDEN:
- Running any script
- Initializing databases
- Registering anchors
- Importing chunks
- Writing to memory.db
- Creating logs or snapshots

DEFAULT STATE:
YES

--------------------------------------------------

STATE 1 ? RECORD

# scripts/state_guard.py
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
STATE_PATH = BASE_DIR / "docs" / "STATE.json"

State = Literal["OBSERVE", "RECORD", "EXECUTE"]

# What is allowed in each state (keep minimal + explicit)
ALLOWED_ACTIONS = {
    "OBSERVE": set(),  # do nothing
    "RECORD": {"snapshot_anchors", "run_script"},
    "EXECUTE": {"snapshot_anchors", "run_script"},
}

def get_state() -> State:
    if not STATE_PATH.exists():
        # STGRAIL rule: If state is undeclared -> do nothing
        raise RuntimeError(f"STATE is undeclared. Missing: {STATE_PATH}")

    data = json.loads(STATE_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    state = data.get("state")
    if state not in ("OBSERVE", "RECORD", "EXECUTE"):
        raise RuntimeError(f"Invalid state in STATE.json: {state}")
    return state  # type: ignore[return-value]

def require_allowed(action: str) -> None:
    state = get_state()
    allowed = ALLOWED_ACTIONS.get(state, set())
    if action not in allowed:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"Blocked by STGRAIL. Current state={state}. Action='{action}' not allowed."
        )

3) Patch scripts/run_recorded.py to require intent + obey state

This version will not run unless:

docs/STATE.json exists

state is RECORD or EXECUTE

--intent "..." is provided

# scripts/run_recorded.py
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from hash_utils import sha256_file
from ops_log import log_event
from state_guard import require_allowed

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DATA_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data"
SCHEMA_PATH = DATA_DIR / "schema.sql"
DB_PATH = DATA_DIR / "memory.db"
LOGS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "logs"

def main() -> int:
    require_allowed("run_script")

    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--intent", required=True, help="Human intent for this run (required).")
    parser.add_argument("script_path", help="Path to the script to run.")
    parser.add_argument("script_args", nargs="*", help="Args passed to the script.")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    script_path = Path(args.script_path).resolve()
    if not script_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Script not found: {script_path}")

    LOGS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    schema_sha = sha256_file(SCHEMA_PATH) if SCHEMA_PATH.exists() else None
    db_sha_before = sha256_file(DB_PATH) if DB_PATH.exists() else None

    cmd = [sys.executable, str(script_path)] + list(args.script_args)
    proc = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)

    run_tag = script_path.stem
    out_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{run_tag}.stdout.log"
    err_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{run_tag}.stderr.log"
    out_path.write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
    err_path.write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")

    db_sha_after = sha256_file(DB_PATH) if DB_PATH.exists() else None

    log_event(
        action="run_script",
        payload={"intent": args.intent, "cmd": cmd},
        artifacts={
            "exit_code": proc.returncode,
            "stdout_path": str(out_path.as_posix()),
            "stderr_path": str(err_path.as_posix()),
            "schema_sha256": schema_sha,
            "db_sha256_before": db_sha_before,
            "db_sha256_after": db_sha_after,
        },
    )

    print(proc.stdout, end="")
    if proc.stderr:
        print(proc.stderr, file=sys.stderr, end="")

    return proc.returncode

if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from hash_utils import sha256_manifest
from ops_log import log_event
from state_guard import require_allowed

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
ANCHORS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "anchors"
SNAP_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data" / "snapshots"


def utc_now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()


def snapshot_anchors() -> Path:
    # ? STGRAIL ENFORCEMENT
    require_allowed("snapshot_anchors")

    SNAP_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    ts = utc_now_iso().replace(":", "").replace("-", "")
    out_path = SNAP_DIR / f"anchors_manifest_{ts}.json"

    manifest = {
        "ts_utc": utc_now_iso(),
        "root": str(ANCHORS_DIR.as_posix()),
        "files": sha256_manifest(ANCHORS_DIR),
    }

    out_path.write_text(
        json.dumps(manifest, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, sort_keys=True),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )

    # ? OPS LEDGER ENTRY (AFTER ACTION PREP)
    log_event(
        action="snapshot_anchors",
        human_intent=(
            "Record a read-only cryptographic snapshot of all anchor files "
            "before any database initialization or ingestion."
        ),
        payload={"anchors_dir": str(ANCHORS_DIR.as_posix())},
        artifacts={
            "manifest_path": str(out_path.as_posix()),
            "file_count": len(manifest["files"]),
        },
    )

    return out_path


if __name__ == "__main__":
    p = snapshot_anchors()
    print(f"[OK] Anchor snapshot written: {p}")

1?? require_allowed()
Must be inside the function

Must be first executable line

Protects against:

CLI execution

imports

programmatic calls

2?? log_event()
Must be after the snapshot is prepared

Must not wrap or contain guards

Logs what happened, not permission checks

if __name__ == "__main__":

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
STATE_PATH = BASE_DIR / "docs" / "STATE.json"

State = Literal["OBSERVE", "RECORD", "EXECUTE"]

# What is allowed in each state (keep minimal + explicit)
ALLOWED_ACTIONS = {
    "OBSERVE": set(),  # do nothing
    "RECORD": {"snapshot_anchors", "run_script"},
    "EXECUTE": {"snapshot_anchors", "run_script"},
}

def get_state() -> State:
    if not STATE_PATH.exists():
        # STGRAIL rule: If state is undeclared -> do nothing
        raise RuntimeError(f"STATE is undeclared. Missing: {STATE_PATH}")

    data = json.loads(STATE_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    state = data.get("state")
    if state not in ("OBSERVE", "RECORD", "EXECUTE"):
        raise RuntimeError(f"Invalid state in STATE.json: {state}")
    return state  # type: ignore[return-value]

def require_allowed(action: str) -> None:
    state = get_state()
    allowed = ALLOWED_ACTIONS.get(state, set())
    if action not in allowed:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"Blocked by STGRAIL. Current state={state}. Action='{action}' not allowed."
        )

ths is sanity check : import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

# -----------------------------
# PATHS
# -----------------------------

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DB_PATH = BASE_DIR / "data" / "memory.db"

# -----------------------------
# SANITY CHECKS
# -----------------------------

def sanity_check():
    if not DB_PATH.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError("[ERROR] memory.db does not exist. Run init_db.py first.")

    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    cursor = conn.cursor()

    try:
        print("? Running sanity checks...\n")

        # 1. Check required tables exist
        required_tables = {
            "anchors",
            "chunks",
            "provenance_notes",
            "runs",
            "run_citations",
        }

        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table';"
        )
        existing_tables = {row[0] for row in cursor.fetchall()}

        missing_tables = required_tables - existing_tables
        if missing_tables:
            raise RuntimeError(f"[ERROR] Missing tables: {missing_tables}")

        print("[OK] All required tables present.")

        # 2. Anchors must exist before chunks
        cursor.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM anchors;")
        anchor_count = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        cursor.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks;")
        chunk_count = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if chunk_count > 0 and anchor_count == 0:
            raise RuntimeError("[ERROR] Chunks exist but no anchors registered.")

        print(f"[OK] Anchors: {anchor_count}, Chunks: {chunk_count}")

        # 3. No NULL import_session_id in anchors or chunks
        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM anchors WHERE import_session_id IS NULL;"
        )
        bad_anchors = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks WHERE import_session_id IS NULL;"
        )
        bad_chunks = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if bad_anchors > 0 or bad_chunks > 0:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"[ERROR] Missing import_session_id "
                f"(anchors: {bad_anchors}, chunks: {bad_chunks})"
            )

        print("[OK] import_session_id present on all anchors and chunks.")

        # 4. Lexicon chunks must have lexicon_word
        cursor.execute(
            """
            SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks
            WHERE truth_type = 'definition'
              AND lexicon_word IS NULL;
            """
        )
        bad_lexicon = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if bad_lexicon > 0:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"[ERROR] {bad_lexicon} lexicon definition chunks missing lexicon_word."
            )

        print("[OK] Lexicon chunks correctly populated.")

        # 5. Chunk IDs must be unique (primary key sanity)
        cursor.execute(
            """
            SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (
                SELECT chunk_id FROM chunks
                GROUP BY chunk_id
                HAVING COUNT(*) > 1
            );
            """
        )
        duplicate_chunks = cursor.fetchone()[0]

        if duplicate_chunks > 0:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"[ERROR] Duplicate chunk_id values detected: {duplicate_chunks}"
            )

        print("[OK] No duplicate chunk IDs detected.")

        print("\n? SANITY CHECK PASSED ? DATABASE IS COHERENT.")

    finally:
        conn.close()


# -----------------------------
# SCRIPT ENTRY POINT
# -----------------------------

    sanity_check()

-- =========================================================

-- =========================================================
CREATE TABLE chunks (
    chunk_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    anchor_id TEXT NOT NULL,

        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    FOREIGN KEY (chunk_id)

OBSERVE-safe fix: paste the canonical content into:

[DIR] scripts/hash_utils.py

Use this (known-good):

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Tuple


def sha256_file(path: Path, chunk_size: int = 1024 * 1024) -> str:
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with path.open("rb") as f:
        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(chunk_size), b""):
            h.update(chunk)
    return h.hexdigest()


def list_files(root: Path) -> List[Path]:
    # Deterministic ordering
    return sorted([p for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file()])


def sha256_manifest(root: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    """
    Returns list of (relative_path, sha256) for every file under root, sorted.
    """
    files = list_files(root)
    out: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
    for f in files:
        rel = f.relative_to(root).as_posix()
        out.append((rel, sha256_file(f)))
    return out
No execution required. Just content restoration.

About memory.db (51 bytes)
This is the kind of tiny thing that causes massive confusion later.

OBSERVE-safe approach (no running, no scripts)

This keeps your ?no shuffle without record? vibe intact.

Your Schema V1.1 update is solid ? one caution
The schema you posted includes a CHECK (...) IN (SELECT ...) inside provenance_notes.

SQLite has limitations around subqueries inside CHECK constraints (varies by version and can behave unexpectedly). In practice, most people enforce that rule at the application layer.

OBSERVE-safe recommendation: keep your schema clean and portable:

use the target_type IN ('anchor','chunk') CHECK [OK]

keep the relational validity enforcement by how you write notes, not by CHECK subqueries

If you keep the subquery check and it works on your SQLite build, fine ? but it might surprise you later on another machine.

# scripts/state_guard.py
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
STATE_PATH = BASE_DIR / "docs" / "STATE.json"

State = Literal["OBSERVE", "RECORD", "EXECUTE"]

# What is allowed in each state (keep minimal + explicit)
ALLOWED_ACTIONS = {
    "OBSERVE": set(),  # do nothing
    "RECORD": {"snapshot_anchors", "run_script"},
    "EXECUTE": {"snapshot_anchors", "run_script"},
}

def get_state() -> State:
    if not STATE_PATH.exists():
        # STGRAIL rule: If state is undeclared -> do nothing
        raise RuntimeError(f"STATE is undeclared. Missing: {STATE_PATH}")

    data = json.loads(STATE_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    state = data.get("state")
    if state not in ("OBSERVE", "RECORD", "EXECUTE"):
        raise RuntimeError(f"Invalid state in STATE.json: {state}")
    return state  # type: ignore[return-value]

def require_allowed(action: str) -> None:
    state = get_state()
    allowed = ALLOWED_ACTIONS.get(state, set())
    if action not in allowed:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"Blocked by STGRAIL. Current state={state}. Action='{action}' not allowed."
        )////// and where should use the target_type IN ('anchor','chunk') CHECK [OK] go

[OK] FIX 2 ? Where the target_type IN ('anchor','chunk') CHECK goes
That CHECK belongs in your SQLite schema, inside the provenance_notes table definition.

[DIR] File
data/schema.sql
[OK] Recommended provenance_notes table (V1.1-safe)
I strongly recommend you keep the simple CHECK and remove the subquery CHECK, because SQLite can be inconsistent with subqueries inside CHECK constraints across builds/versions (and it can bite you later on a different machine).

Use this:

CREATE TABLE provenance_notes (
    note_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    target_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (target_type IN ('anchor', 'chunk')),
    target_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    note TEXT NOT NULL,
    author TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL
);
Why this is the right place
target_type is the polymorphic selector (?what kind of thing is target_id referring to??)

The CHECK constraint belongs right on that column, in the table that stores it

How to enforce ?target_id must exist?
Because it?s polymorphic (anchor OR chunk), you can?t do a normal foreign key cleanly. The best pattern is:

enforce via script-level validation when inserting provenance notes, e.g.:

if target_type == 'anchor' -> verify anchors.anchor_id = target_id

if target_type == 'chunk' -> verify chunks.chunk_id = target_id

That keeps the DB portable and the logic explicit.
